  because she copies lists and does not read them. Ning Yan says the sentence out loud once in the
  wash house: **by the county.** Not thanked, does not thank. Three washerwomen heard it; by the end of the week
  it is in a market, for nothing. **The county's written request for the season's furnace schedule
  goes up the quarter's return sheet on the girl's own hour, entered by her, into the crate at ch 129.**
  **Alive. Do not kill her.**
- **The relicensing mechanism (ch 124, used thereafter).** A second application down the same three
  legs, with an hours record **continuous from the day the tithe book opened** (the origin date ch 115
  reads aloud). Condition two is the door: hire hours are not furnace hours and no amendment can put
  them there. **The annex is the way round — hours written onto the Qinghe furnace schedule by the
  Hall's copyist, certified by the Hall's clerk, stamped by a maintaining member, and returned at the
  quarter, are furnace-schedule hours by certification and not by place. Legal since the clause was
  written. Nobody in nine counties has ever paid for it before.** The annex puts her hours into the
  quota book and unlevels a line that has been level since the middle of the spring. **The claim
  rides on the new stamp, entered last, because Sun He is required to enter it.**
- **The Hunter's first purchase (ch 126 — batch midpoint).** **A certified copy of the Qinghe furnace
  schedule**, coin on open wood, at the public counter, at her county hour, eight feet from her while
  she signs her own hours in her own hand. He reads the whole of it there, stops on the stage column
  (one, two, three, the gap, five), and says two true things to a young clerk of four years:
  **the next stage must be announced or the paper fails its own terms**, and **a licensee capped at
  five cannot be examined past five.** The clerk half agrees with him **aloud**. **Sun He hears both
  and says *he is right about the stage column* while writing the hour.** The line goes up in the
  quarter's bundle on the twenty-fourth and Sun He is **required** to tell the maintaining members.
  **He buys one thing in the whole batch and it is this, and he keeps the receipt, folded square.**
  He lies to nobody, threatens nobody, touches nobody, enters no room, pays for no force, and is not
  from the Bureau, which is true.
- **His room (ch 127).** **Over the tanner's, four rooms, nine years.** A strip of sacking on the door
  carries the name against the tallow render and the lamp oil, written by a woman who cannot read, from
  the lodger's own mouth: **Chou Zhyan — a clerk's misspelling with the doubled letter in it**, made
  from memory with no form in the room. She recognises it because the Hall's copyist has made the
  opposite mistake for a year. **She does not go up the stairs.** He has stood on Feng's fourth stair
  five times since the spring and has **never once asked for anything**. **No audit of Feng, no Lung
  offered or refused, column at five, four county copies still walled behind the fifth item.**
- **The quiet book (ch 128) — the batch's irreversible decision, and it is a decision not to act.**
  She states the condition aloud in the corridor — *if I use it, the seal is bought, and a bought
  signature is a purchase and not a sponsorship* — and they say **then don't**, and she doesn't.
  **Nothing about the sponsors' second book, the nine struck households, the eleven-year schedule, the
  fourth line citing the maintaining members' ledger, or the frame of twelve unsigned sheets is lodged,
  copied or spoken anywhere in ch 119–178. The seal was bought with silence.** She can never go back to
  not-knowing that they know she knows. **The sponsors' silence on the writ is now purchased and they
  go on saying nothing.**
- **The crate (ch 129).** Day 28, **on the day and not the day before.** The Hall clerk writes her
  **name in the wrong order on the certification — the one permitted use in this batch, and
  unexplained — and Lu Kang reads it and does not correct it.** The county reads a crate **in the
  order it is stacked**; she works that out, does not tell a sixty-one-year-old keeper how to do his
  job, and gets the schedule onto the top and a list in her own hand onto the very top of that.
  **Feng coughs on the stair and does not offer to carry anything.** She will not put a paper crate on
  a cart. A boy of seventeen runs one finger down four months of her column and finds nothing.
  **The road is OPEN throughout this batch.** The crate day does not move.
- **The second clearance (ch 130).** Shen Xiu asks his **first question in a year**: *whose hours are
  in the annex, yours alone or the crew's.* She answers hers alone. **He will not weight a figure for
  one arm, will not close a season early, and makes her write the asked figure in her own hand: fifty-
  eight hours in the seven weeks left.** He signs **in one stroke, without looking up**, which he has
  never done for anybody. **He states the ch 34 early report out loud, unasked, under the desk.** She
  says *I have not forgiven you* and is not thanked. **Something close to respect, which she does not
  convert into anything.** The frame of twelve unsigned sheets is on the back wall and **is not
  examined**.
- **Three marks (ch 131).** Pei An reads it standing, Deng Chao reads it out, Lu Kang asks four
  questions **and none of them is about her intentions**, and then says out loud, for the record, that
  it is the hardest signature of his life and that he is signing it anyway, because the alternative is a
  member handed over with her schedule in a hand and **this Hall is a book that has been opened.**
  **She writes her own ceiling for the third time in the book: Condensing nine**, re-enters her own
  defect (*aperture cracked through; stabilised at the crack; permanent*) and adds one new line under
  it: ***the defect restores nothing to the aperture. It is entered here again because this is a new
  document and a document carries what it carries.*** She thanks nobody.
- **The relicensing granted (ch 132).** Sun He **stands up and reads the application in order, out
  loud, to a room.** **Seven conditions on the back**: the six carried forward, the **second amended**
  so that the certified annex is furnace hours by certification and not another schedule, plus **one
  new sixth condition** — the annex hours return with the quarter, on the same crate and in the same
  order as the schedule they are annexed to, and an annex that is not returned is not on the schedule.
  **The eight hundred jin is the seventh and the last thing on the paper**, entered because he is
  required to. He does not say *you may pick up your licence* this time and she notices. He says the
  licence is worth exactly a stamp and the seventh condition is the reason that is true and not a lie.
  **Do not re-list the seven conditions in any chapter after this one — reference, never re-list.**
- **Titles 119–132:** The Hire Sheet, Where the Work Is, The Nineteen Minutes, The First Desk, One True
  Sentence, The Second Application, The Dead Writ, The Certified Copy, Under a Misspelling, The Quiet
  Book, The Order of the Crate, Level With the Asked, Nine in Her Own Hand, Relicensed. All unique
  against 1–118.
- **Chapter length:** 36,809 words of body prose over 14 chapters, **average ~2,629 — inside the 1,800–2,700
  guide on the average.** Six sit individually above the guide and eight inside: ch 126 (2,748), 127
  (2,761), 129 (2,916), 130 (2,973), 131 (2,750) and 132 (3,002) — the public lane, the dread of
  being correct, the misspelling, the crate, the clearance, the three marks and the countersignature,
  all of which are complete scenes. **The first record of this batch said eleven of the fourteen were
  individually inside and named only three long ones; that was wrong and is corrected here.** A second
  record counted full files including `##` headings and so listed **seven**, with ch 120 at 2,710; on body
  prose ch 120 is 2,689 and comes inside. **Counts are body prose only.** Nothing
  under the 1,300 floor; no monotone decline. No trade-off recorded. **Do not read the average as a
  precedent either way — write each chapter to its scene.**
- **New minor character:** **Da Shan**, the man at the board in the hiring lane. Not a villain, not an
  ally, no function beyond the hire. He says his deduction out loud so that it is clear it came off one
  woman and nobody else, and the batch keeps him for that.

### Construction budget — spent in Batch 0001, not to reuse

The six licence conditions as written out (ch 115–116, re-listed once more at ch 132 **as a fresh
document with seven** — do not write them again). *There is no form* (cut entirely at drafting, 0
uses). *There is no column* (2 uses, once each at ch 124 and 127, different meanings). The suppression
arithmetic (ch 115, used at ch 126 **by the Hunter, correctly, in one paragraph — do not run it
again**). The suppression **consequence** for the filing is used at ch 131 and ch 132 in one line
each. *Nobody asked*, *nobody was thanked*, *the record remained*, *nobody knew*: **zero.** The four
seconds (ch 53–54): **not used as a beat**; ordinary elapsed time appears six times and the numeral four
was cut from two of them. Authorial scaffolding: **zero.** `his eyes widened`, `he couldn't believe it`,
`suddenly`, `little did he know`: **zero.**

### Carried forward, unchanged

Crew **nine men; quarters sleep ten in twelve bunks.** The girl of twenty-four alive. Hou Sanliang
unpaid, patient, **never in a room with her, never met** — he does not appear in this batch. Feng alive,
unaudited, coughing, column at **five**, **no Lung offered or refused**, four county copies walled behind
the fifth item, the volumes on the high shelf not copies and not struck. Shen Xiu unforgiven, unthanked.
The three sponsors silent on a dead, unrevoked Foundation writ. Register still reads *reconciled*;
**the plate is still worn**; the fourth stage of her life is still on no paper in nine counties and
never will be. *Kiln-born* in two heads and no paper; the office name still unreadable. Surety void,
debt hers. The twelve-sheet frame logged and untouched. The tower intake line at the ninth hour, not
audited. **She does not forgive him** — stated once at ch 121 and running into Volume 4.

## Established facts, Volume 3 Batch 0002 (ch 133–148) — AUDIT AND MERCY

Sixteen chapters, **36,742 words**, average ~2,296. **Day 0 is the last day of the thaw** (canon
change 30). Ch 132 = day 39; **this batch runs day 42 to about day 130**, and Batch 0003 opens at about
**day 133**. Chapter days: 133 = 42 · 134 = 48 · 135 = 48→81 (spans) · 136 = 83 · 137 = 86 · 138 = 89 ·
139 = 90 · 140 = 92 · 141 = 95 · 142 = 96 · 143 = 97 · 144 = 99 · 145 = 104 · 146 = 110 · 147 = 114 ·
148 = 116→130 (spans). **The pacing inside the batch is uneven on purpose** — ch 133–135 carry six
weeks, the other thirteen carry five.

**Two day seams against that fixed table, recorded and not re-dated.** The outline forbids re-dating
without reconciling every elapsed figure, and in both cases the page is self-consistent while the
table is the narrower of the two, so the table is the thing that is slightly short rather than the
prose being wrong. **Do not "correct" either of these in Batch 0003, and do not build a figure on top of
them.** (1) **Ch 143 closes on day 99**, not inside the 93–97 span, because he signs at the ninth hour of
day 97 and the Listener's first return is two days later — day 99 is also ch 144's own day, so the two
chapters share it. (2) **Ch 147 has the season's road shut on day 117**, one day past the 97–116 span,
and she learns the date at the weigh-station on day 114. **Day 117 is carried into Batch 0003's prompt
and into `state/current.md` and must not move.**

**Power: Condensing 5 → 6 at ch 135, announced at her order; Condensing 6 → 7 at ch 148, announced at
her order. Nothing else. No tribulation. Three bracketed layer lines in the batch, one each in ch 135,
ch 140 and ch 148, fresh four-clause wording, none reusing ch 53 / 78 / 92 / 114.**

- **Two clocks (ch 133–134).** Furnace floor at Qinghe from the ninth hour + the lane behind a well at
  Lowwater + **four miles of road between them on cleared cart days** + county hour at the third hour.
  **Four loads and eight coppers gone every county hour** — a quarter of a day's pay. Crew nine, ten
  sleepers, twelve bunks. Da Shan and the board carry over.
  - **Canon change 34 — the irregular annex.** The Hall's copyist had been certifying the annex by
    converting Da Shan's **loads** at the Hall's two-armed rate. At day 42 the column read **31 hours
    against the 58 she had asked in her own hand at ch 130**. She got it certified in hours she wrote
    out herself, every day, in the order the days happened, which is an **irregular entry**: the Hall
    clerk writes ***irregular*** on the front of the Qinghe furnace schedule and stamps it. **Nine men
    read it on the furnace floor the same afternoon** (the crew reads its own schedule) and one of them,
    Gao, asks; she answers in one sentence and goes back to the cold end. **Cost: an hour of the
    furnace day every day, carried four miles to a stamp.**
  - **Canon change 35 — the scope line's third half, read at last (ch 134).** A member licensed to
    practise past registration **may be taught past her stage, under countersignature, at that county.**
    She had carried the licence four months without noticing. She does the arithmetic in eleven minutes
    and concludes that no master in nine counties will put his hand to it (a sponsor touching it again
    breaks the silence bought at ch 128; a stranger touching it puts her in an office's line in order),
    and **decides to build the stage herself, out of load, on the work** — which is the thing ch 140's
    tax then takes.
- **Stage six (ch 135).** Six weeks, **240 loads**, forty-one stamps, arm at a third throughout, four
  loads a county hour every time. Arrives at the water channel at the third hour of day 81.
  **Announced at her own order at the fourth hour.** The announcement says ***at Lowwater***, and there
  is one building in that town where a sentence can be turned into a door, and her licence with her
  annex address is in it. **A Lowwater counting room has opened its book again** — told to her by the
  girl of twenty-four off a copied carriers' slate with **twelve household lines** on the back of it and
  a stamp that is neither the county's nor the Hall's; the man who brought it counted the girl's slate
  with his thumb, paid her, and wrote a name on a slip she did not read. **Hou Sanliang does not appear
  and is not met.** The girl is alive and not thanked.
- **The audit (ch 136–140), which is what the Hunter's lawful request buys.**
  - **The request (ch 136).** Served in person on the copying floor, seventh visit, knocked on the frame
    for the first time. Ordinary county stationery, a county mark, a stamp she has no name for. It asks
    for the paper the stair carries upward for the quarter, **in the form in which it is carried**, and
    carries six words in a margin in an unfamiliar hand: ***including what is not entered***. It is
    lawful, stamped, complete and exactly right. **A keeper cannot refuse it, delay it, or answer it
    partly.** She works out in four seconds that she could stop it at the county and says **no** out
    loud to nothing, because a request carries a reason and a reason goes in a book with her name in
    the first line. **The request is for the stair, not for the line: the intake hour at the ninth is
    on the form and is NOT examined as a change.**
  - **The answer (ch 137).** Written in one sitting, five sheets, fair hand. The working lines; four
    hundred and eleven volumes by shelf and number; **the column at five for twenty-two years**; the
    list of the four certified county copies delivered in the thaw, walled behind the fifth item,
    entered upward for no quarter — **one of them struck**; and as item five **the ch 117 fair line at
    the back of a fair volume**, which is the only line about her in nine counties. **She does not ask
    him to leave it out.** Irreversible. He tells her she should have stopped it and he is glad she did
    not, and both are true.
  - **The finding (ch 138).** A boy of seventeen with a finger and about forty minutes. The return says:
    returned five sheets against an expected six; missing item, the entry of the four copies. **No
    office invents a column and nobody complains.**
  - **The strike (ch 139).** Copy three, undated, unattributed. Three clerks and a ledger from the high
    shelf: **four hundred and sixty entries in eleven years, one hundred and nine from outside the
    bound, every one of them from an office a county can put on a line — and one that it cannot.** Sun
    He writes the line himself and tells the clerk to enter no inference. Feng is questioned about dates
    and **is not asked to recognise a hand**; he discloses the request, the shape-not-the-word rule and
    the fact that he observed the striking in the thaw and reported nothing, because the county's own
    copy was not struck. **This is a fact the county finds on a copy, not a second request Feng serves.
    It is not a second strike action and the office is not named.**
  - **The suspension (ch 139).** One line: the keeper of the Lantern Register at Lowwater is suspended
    from the compilation and entry of the quarterly upward bundle **for one quarter**. The bundle is
    carried by a clerk of the county office. **The volumes and the copying floor remain in his hands.
    The column remains in his hands and is not altered. Nothing removes him from his office, his
    stipend, his volumes or his house. The district does not terminate an examination.** The four copies
    are taken into the county office in a satchel and read at its table. **He is not dismissed.**
- **The listed kill (ch 140).** On the escort road below the fourth bridge, a bounty man in good boots
  working an entered act **reads a carrier's boy of fourteen his own description out loud** in the flat
  voice of a man doing his work, the way the ch 101 man read Du Hong's file. He says plainly that he is
  working the act, that he is not from the Bureau, and that he needs a place, a county and a name, and
  that he has eight proofs left. **She sends the boy up the grade.**
  - **Canon change 36 — the escort-road pace, and what it was.** Five weeks of a road with a train on
    it and a boy on the ground built a **sequence**: the point at which a wheel begins to lean, a quarter
    of a mile before it arrives. **Told before spent.** Out of her foundation, does not return, takes
    **sequence not substance**, and **cannot be relearned**. She kills him with the quarterstaff in her
    left hand, one working arm, **no order left in her hands at all**, on her reading of a man's
    shoulders, and very nearly dies — the cold opens, she loses two seconds, and he gets to the third
    cart.
  - **A man working an entered act who is not himself on one settles, but small**, because the layer
    settles against the working and not against the man, and there is no name and no act against him.
    **Bracket.** **Channel: about three weeks → about ten weeks.**
  - **She tells the Watch the whole of it flat and does not soften it and puts no cause in it that is
    not hers.** A boy's lead line goes in as a thing found on the body. **The eight hundred jin stands
    exactly where it stood.** **A second body in this county with her hand on it.**
  - **The Hunter buys nothing and is not involved.** The ch 68 entered-act arithmetic is **used, not
    restated**.
- **The Lung (offered ch 141, taken ch 143).**
  - **Canon change 37 — the Listener's appointment, four lines.** *Reports the Lantern Register's paper
    upward truly and in full, at the quarter, in the form in which it is carried, **whether or not it is
    entered, and whether or not the keeper's hand is in it**.* *An omission by the Listener is an
    omission by the office and is entered against the office and not against the keeper.* *The
    appointment is for life.* ***It may be set aside on request from without the bound, without the
    consent of the Listener, upon the same terms as a designation.*** In exchange: a district wage
    quarterly, oil for the lamp, a hundredweight of coal to the door in the first week of the cold, and
    **a chair in the county office on the county's reading days at the second hour with the county's
    fire.** **The offer is not from the Bureau** and the hunter is not from the Bureau, which is true.
    **The county's own book has nothing for it and nothing against it, and the appointment is not entered
    in the county's register.**
  - **The Hunter offers it himself, in the copying room, and says three things: the hook is not hidden
    and is not in the county's book; nobody is being bought and there is no coin in the room; and he did
    not choose the keeper — a county examined a tower because a stranger asked a lawful question, and
    there is a member of this county in the sixth stage whose hours are on two of the five sheets, and
    he has not mentioned her.** **He never asks Feng for the word.**
  - **He asks for one night** (ch 141), walks four miles to the county office to read the county's own
    book and finds nothing (ch 142), and **signs at the ninth hour on day 97 with her standing at the end
  of the bench** (ch 143). He says he is sixty-two and will be dead before it goes wrong and she will not.
  - **THE COST, STATED ON THE PAGE: from that hour every hour she copies goes upward truly, and the
    Hunter stops needing to ask, because a Listener reports the whole of the stair and needs no request.**
    His first return, on day 99, is the bundle's contents with a fresh line at the top: *Appointed this
    quarter. Reporting in full from the next.* **She is the reason there is something to report. She
    cannot un-take it, she does not ask him not to take it, and she does not forgive him.**
  - **Feng takes ONE Lung. The second is Volume 11 and is not spent here.** He survives, coughing less,
    writing more, owned. Column at five, unchanged.
- **Escort road and stage seven (ch 144–148).**
  - **Canon change 38 — the guard's hire.** Twenty coppers a day and a **quarter of the train's share**
    (about one whole copper), five weeks to the close of the season's road, down to the fourth bridge
    and back with the trains, eight men on a train and a ninth on the bridge, **the road shuts with the
    weather and not with the contractor**. Escort work is paid work outside the crew and therefore
    **inside Clause Four's scope**, and therefore **has to be annexed by the Hall's copyist, certified,
    and stamped**, exactly like everything else. Paid at the two-armed rate and worked at a third of it.
  - **New minor character: Tan**, the contractor's hired guard, paid out of his own pocket, about
    thirty, nine years on the road, a bar shorter than her staff. Not a villain, not an ally, no
    function beyond the road. **He catches the wheel at ch 144 and tells her not to tell the contractor
    why, which is a decision he has already made.** He is the reason she is still on the road.
  - **The pace is gone and the road does not tell her.** The first thing she loses is the ability to
    know where a wheel is going, and she finds out from a caught wheel, not from a body.
  - **Canon change 39 — the second hand.** Escort loads have no hour on them. A **carriers' run list**,
    entered by the weigh-house rather than by whoever holds the slate, is a second hand the county
    accepts. **The girl of twenty-four has been copying about two hundred run lists since the thaw for
    eight coppers a sheet and nobody has ever wanted one.** Ning Yan pays eight coppers a sheet for four
    sheets, **on the day she hands them over and not before**, says why out loud, does not thank her, and
    is stopped from saying thank you by the girl in advance. The girl is alive, untouchable, and not
    thanked, and this is her payment for that arrangement.
  - **Stage seven (ch 148).** Built out of load and watching, **and out of the opposite thing to stage
    six** — not knowing in advance, but being wrong and going back and looking. Arrives on the fourth
    night of a fever at about the second hour. **Announced at her order. Bracket.** **Cost: two weeks
    of fever, eleven days in the bed, and the short breath deepening from two counts to THREE COUNTS on
    stairs, which never comes back** — first found at the top of the third flight of the crew quarters
    about nine days after the fever broke.
- **Other batch 0002 facts.**
  - **She declines the county carrier** on the physician's instruction, in the three words a form allows
    for, and is carried down four flights by a man from the Hall's own door who owes a furnace crew a
    lamp in the thaw. Neither mentions it.
  - **The season's road closed on day 117** and nobody announced it; the contractor's chalk was simply
    not on the slate. **The lane behind a well has about four weeks left in it and then there is no
    paid work in this county for a licensed woman with one arm until the thaw.**
  - **The column comes out level at the close: fifty-eight asked and fifty-eight worked**, stamped by
    the Hall clerk at the second hour, who writes ***asked and worked level at the close*** and not
    *complete*, because complete is for an ordinary member. **The relicensing is now paid for in hours
    that actually happened.**
  - **The second licence is still not nailed up.** The first is still crooked over the foot of her own
    bunk, going soft at the crease of the defect line. The second is in oilcloth against her ribs above
    the plate. That is the ch 178 image and it is untouched.
  - **Feng has not asked her a question since the appointment** and has worked out why himself; she
    worked out that he had, nine days late. He copies her upward for four days in a fair hand while she
    lies in the loft and **she does not go down.**
  - **She does not forgive him.** Non-forgiveness runs into Volume 4.
- **Titles 133–148:** The Two Clocks, The Annex Is Work, Six, The Lawful Request, A Keeper Answers,
  The Four Behind the Fifth, A Name Nobody Keeps, Paid in Sequence, Oil for the Cough, A Tower He Cannot
  Feed, Mercy With a Hook, A Hired Guard, Loaded and Watched, Under Licence Scope, The Last Load, Three
  Counts. All unique against 1–132.
- **Chapter length:** 36,742 words of body prose over 16 chapters, **average ~2,296**. **Two sit above
  the 1,800–2,700 guide** (135 at 2,792 · 140 at 3,193, and 140 is the batch's climax) and the other
  fourteen inside it; shortest is ch 138 at 1,779. Nothing is under the 1,300 floor and there is no
  monotone decline. No trade-off recorded. **Do not read the average as a precedent — write each chapter
  to its scene.**
- **Word-count convention, stated once:** every figure in the state files counts **body prose only —
  everything outside the `##` section headings.** An earlier record counted full files, which inflated
  every figure by the length of the headings; Batch 0001's 37,177 is 36,809 on this convention and
  Batch 0002's 37,063 is 36,742. Both are corrected here. Use body prose for anything new.

### Construction budget — spent in Batch 0002, not to reuse

The seven licence conditions as written out: **zero** (referenced, never re-listed). Shen Xiu's fifty-eight
and the fact that a gap is a gap: used, **not explained**. The suppression arithmetic: **zero**. The
claim-as-entered-act arithmetic (ch 68): **used once at ch 140, not restated.** *There is no form*: **zero.
There is no column*: **zero — batch 0001 spent its two uses and neither is repeated.** *Nobody asked*,
*nobody was thanked*, *the record remained*, *nobody knew*: **zero.** The sealed slip's
position-not-characters rule: **disclosed once by Feng as part of the audit answer and not re-run as a
rule**; no second request is served by anybody. Authorial scaffolding: **zero**. `his eyes widened`,
`he couldn't believe it`, `suddenly`, `little did he know`: **zero**. Weekdays: **zero named anywhere in
the batch** (two were caught in draft and cut). Bold and italic: used only on documents being read
aloud, on the word *irregular*, and twice for emphasis. **No bare Settlement fraction on the page** —
weeks only, said at ch 132 and at ch 140 and not between.

### Carried forward, unchanged, into Batch 0003

Crew **nine men; quarters sleep ten in twelve bunks.** The girl of twenty-four alive, four paid hours,
**now holding a quarter of a slab and two months of uncopied run lists in her own ledger**. Hou Sanliang
unpaid, patient, **never in a room with her, never met** — he does not appear in the batch and his
counting room is a fact on a copied slate. Shen Xiu does not appear. The three sponsors do not appear and
their purchased silence holds — **nothing about the second book is lodged, copied or spoken anywhere in
ch 119–178.** The register still reads *reconciled*; **the plate is still worn**; the fourth stage of her
life is on no paper in nine counties and never will be. *Kiln-born* in two heads and no paper; the office
name still unreadable and **not named in this batch**. Surety void, debt hers. The twelve-sheet frame
logged and untouched. **The tower intake line at the ninth hour is not audited and not examined as a
change.** **The Foundation writ is dead and not revoked and the relicensing did not revive it. Nothing
about ch 149 is foreshadowed, named or touched.** **She does not forgive him.**

## Established facts, Volume 3 Batch 0003 (ch 149–163) — HUNTING WITH A CALENDAR

Fifteen chapters, **30,961 words**, average ~2,064. **Day 0 is the last day of the thaw.**
Ch 148 = about day 130; **this batch runs day 133 to about day 184.** Chapter days: 149 = 133 · 150 = 136 ·
151 = 139 · 152 = 140 · 153 = 147 · 154 = 150 · 155 = 153 · 156 = 156 · 157 = 158 · 158 = 161 · 159 = 164 ·
160 = 166→175 (spans) · 161 = 178 · 162 = 181 · 163 = 184.

**Two day seams, both recorded so Batch 0004 does not "correct" them.** (1) **The two monthly attendances are
day 147 and day 178.** The standing order says *a month* and **no chapter states the gap between them in
days** — do not add one. (2) **The quarry carrying begins on day 140** (ch 152) and five weeks of it end on
**day 175**; the suppression is **the thirty-third day of the quarry, day 172**, and **the page never calls
it a week of anything.** Ch 160 spans 166→175 and closes the carrying.

**Power: Condensing 7 → 8 at ch 160, SUPPRESSED at her own order. NOTHING ELSE. No tribulation.
ZERO bracketed layer lines in the whole batch, and ch 160 carries none by rule — a suppressed stage reads
as nothing having happened and no absence-line, silence-line or substitute for the bracket is written
anywhere in 149–163.**

### The milestone (ch 149–150) — the volume's irreversible decision

- **The Ledger break (ch 149), on her own word, in public.** The handover paper has been on the mat of the
  tower loft since day 129, folded twice, **not addressed, not sealed, and signed by no person.** She could
  not verify a document with her eyes and cannot read a man at all, so on **day 133 she says it out loud on
  the copying floor in front of Feng Lushi** — *I am going to have him read a man through my eyes* — and has
  him enter it. **He writes it as a TOWER CHANGE, not a request** (a thing that happened in the building,
  not a service rendered to her), in fair hand, on the day's sheet, with both the asking and the doing in
  it, and it goes up the quarter. **The price is named out loud before the word is said: four and a half
  weeks out of about ten.** Ledger held **under a day**.
  - **What the reading bought:** the room over the tanner's; **a board of dates in his own hand**; **the
    eight remaining proofs in order**; **the quarterly return already sent upward with her hours, her annex,
    the irregular stamp and the stage column one, two, three, five, six, seven in it**; and **the four
    sheets of the handover verified true, line by line.**
  - **What it did not buy:** **the office name. The eleven. Design.** All three refused in the same
    breath.
  - **He refuses to hold it again the same day — permanently for the volume.** She does not thank him and
    does not forgive him.
- **The handover (ch 150), read, refused, signed, entered. THE VOLUME'S IRREVERSIBLE DECISION.**
  - **She moves the table out of his room into the county office on the square**, and he agrees without a
    word of argument, and says plainly that he would rather she had it in a room with no clerk in it because
    everything said in that building is written down twice. **A county office writes down what is said in
    it — she chose the room so that the refusal would be on a record he does not control.**
  - **Sun He enters the hearing in the ordinary hand** (hour, room, the fact that a member asked and named
    the reason) and reads the four sheets at the third hour. They are headed and signed **by the Warden's
    office**, carry a number, and **carry no agent's name.**
  - **Canon change 41 — the four terms, all true.** (1) The Bureau certifies the voice as a **misread
    natural echo** on a named day before the end of the winter. (2) The designation is **drawn down over
    one winter, without injury and without a stage being taken from her** — the only term about her body on
    the sheet. (3) **The licence stands**, terms, ceiling, conditions, defect and seventh condition all as
    they are. (4) **The eight hundred jin is answered out of the Warden's own purse**, entered in this
    county as a standing claim satisfied by a third party, which **pays Hou Sanliang in full on the day**
    and stops the claim standing and stops it travelling on the licence.
  - **Canon change 42 — the two questions, and true answers.** *Who pays:* the Warden's office, its own
    purse; **not this county, not the three sponsors**; a thing counties write two or three times a decade;
    the money goes to the creditor. *What happens to the thing in her head:* **it goes out**, on a date
    inside the winter; and **he cannot tell her what becomes of it and says so** — *I do not know, neither
    does anyone in this room, the sheet does not know either* — and adds that it does not hurt, which is a
    term and not a comfort.
  - **Canon change 43 — the fourth line of the second sheet.** *Announcements already carried to the bound
    are not comprised in this instrument and are not affected by it.* Plain, in the ordinary place, not
    hidden, written by a man who did not put it in and would not have taken it out. **This is why she
    refuses**, and the refusal is not stubbornness: the mercy is real and it would work and it pays a
    creditor she has never met, and it leaves four announcements standing in nine counties with nobody left
    alive to explain them.
  - **She refuses aloud, in front of two witnesses, and signs the refusal in her own left hand** — three
    lines, the date, her name — and says she wants it in a county's hand and that she was told the terms
    were as read and that she verified them **yesterday by her own means** and is not repeating how.
  - **Canon change 44 — Bai Zhu, named once.** The clerk brings **a Warden's book out of a press under the
    counter** — not this county's book, kept in this county, one of two, the other at the cross, looked at
    twice a year by a man from the district who never writes in either. The form wants the **agent who laid
    the paper**. The paper carries none. **Bai Zhu is named here, by the agent, to a clerk, once.** The
    clerk writes it, and **Sun He writes it into the county book as well**, in the ordinary hand, because a
    county writes down what is said in it. **He is not named in any other chapter of the book, and never on
    a document he signs.**
  - **Standing after ch 150 and untouched by 151–163:** the refusal is on her **member's sheet**, which is
    copied at the quarter and at any application and **goes up with whatever else goes up — the Qinghe
    furnace schedule, to nine men who read their own schedule.** **Hou Sanliang is still unpaid and still
    patient and is still never in a room with her.** The eight hundred jin still stands and still travels.
    **She does not thank Yuan for the reading and does not forgive him for the price of it — both on the
    page, once, in that order.**

### Hunting with a calendar (ch 151–163)

- **Canon change 45 — the monthly attendance.** The district's standing order under a refusal entered in a
  Warden's book: a member who has declined an instrument laid by an officer of that office **attends that
  office once a month**, in person, signed, on a fixed day, at the third hour. Entered by Sun He without
  comment — **she is the first this office has had.** It **does not touch the licence**; it is on the
  member's sheet. **A missed attendance is entered separately from her hours and cannot be paid for in
  hours.** Cost per month: one morning, a quarter of a day's pay, plus four miles.
- **Canon change 46 — the monthly reading.** A quarterly return is signed; **a monthly attendance is read**
  and then signed, because a sheet is copied at the quarter and at any application. Sun He reads the stage
  column aloud and **states the county's ruling — an absence is not a breach — to the room and not to her,
  the second time, with the Hunter present, and enters it again.** He is not defending her; he is putting
  the ruling on the record beside a man who said the opposite thing at a public counter in June. **Both
  sentences are now in the same county's book and no clerk can write down both of them being right.**
- **The quarry (ch 152, 154, 157–163).** Three miles east on the Verge road, eleven men, a hoist worked by
  four by hand, **eighteen coppers a load**, loads off the cut on a sledge with no wheel on the near side,
  a bar behind the shoulder. **New minor characters: Bo**, the foreman, about fifty, one question asked of
  her in the whole batch; and **Kan**, the hoist-man, about forty, who writes the cards and is the reason
  the quarry hours exist on paper at all. **Bo does not learn about the hand and is not written as caring;
  Kan is owed nothing by anyone and is owed something by her.**
- **Canon change 47 — the signing hand (ch 154).** The heel of her **left** hand at the base of the thumb,
  off a sledge handle while lifting a load off a man on ice. She can hold; **she cannot make the tight
  rotation at the end of a stroke**, so about a third of every load's pay goes and does not come back.
  **A county hour is in person and in her own hand, so a ruined hand is a filing problem.** She rebuilds
  the whole column **with the flat of the second finger** — no wrist turn, every figure finished by a drag
  out to the right. Slower, legible, hers. Nine minutes to find it on a slate, about nine days to be able to
  do it without stopping, and **eleven minutes longer on every county hour afterwards, which nobody in nine
  counties will ever know.** The escort-road pace is gone and is not explained again — one clause, because a
  wheel is on the page.
- **Canon change 48 — the weight ticket (ch 158).** The lane had **carriers' run lists entered by the
  weigh-house**, the county's accepted second hand. **The quarry has none**: Bo called the number off the
  rope and the hoist-man wrote it on a card, and weight is not time. **A quarry hour was therefore
  uncertifiable, so the annex did not fill, and a gap in that column is work done and not shown — which is
  not an absence.** She stands at the rope three days and **Kan writes the hour in at the left and the
  load in at the right on one line**, at the cost of being the man holding the pen when a foreman is asked
  about a card. Forty-one cards, irregular, stamped — the word is on the front of the Qinghe furnace
  schedule for the fourth time in a year.
- **The dated claim (ch 155).** The girl of twenty-four brings a copied slate: the twelve household lines
  on the front, **and six words and a date on the back in a hand that is not the hand on the front and a
  better pen.** **A counting room does not enter a future date against a claim it is content to wait on.**
  It is a date to **answer by**, for entry for execution, with everything required already on file. **A
  date turns a claim into a schedule.** The girl is paid eight coppers on the day, is not thanked, and says
  out loud that she has been put under the line and asks for it to be recorded between them.
  **Hou Sanliang does not appear and is not met.**
- **The boy (ch 156).** **Ruan, and he is fifteen, not fourteen** — he got in off the down train, four days
  a week at a carrier's yard and a day washing cloth, **owed four coppers and paid three.** He gave the man
  a road and a county because the man had hold of his ear and asked nicely, and he says *I thought you
  would be a bigger one. You read small.* He is in the Watch's book in one line, as **a witness to an
  entered act**, and that is the only line in nine counties that says he saw anything. **She does not give
  him money** — money on a carrier's boy gets written on the back of something — and instead says out loud
  in the yard, to a clerk with a good hand, in front of eleven men, that the description is about her and
  not about him. **She does not go back, and says so to him first.** **The ch 140 body's county entry is
  not reopened and the claim's proof arithmetic is not restated.**
- **The crew (ch 159, 162).** **She tells nine men about the refusal on a furnace floor a fortnight before a
  clerk's hand would**, and **Gao's whole response is to stand on her other side at the grate for four
  hours and never mention it.** The winter crate reaches the Hall, the schedule goes on the board, **nine
  men read it and say nothing at all** — the silence she spent nine days in the summer and six weeks in
  the winter being afraid of, and it was cheaper than she had priced it.
- **Canon change 49 — Da Shan (ch 162).** A carrier tells her that a stranger stood at the fourth table in
  the autumn and **read the crew's own sheet off the wall, all the way down**, the way he read the certified
  copy off a counter in the summer. **Da Shan gave it to him.** He had no reason not to; the sheet was on
  his wall and a man asked to read it. **The mechanism is a word she put on the front of a schedule
  herself.** She answers it by putting **four of her crew on his lane on their own time, without asking
  him**; **Gao tells Da Shan her name when he asks the second time** (she told him he would and he did);
  **Da Shan puts the frame back on the wall himself and never asks her a single question.** The board has
  **six frames out of eight** through the cold. **Da Shan is minor and is not forgiven, thanked or
  questioned.**
- **Canon change 50 — the suppressed stage (ch 160).** Five weeks of winter-quarry carrying with the stage
  inside her the whole way; **it is not a weight, it is an address.** The grief worked is the boy of
  fourteen, now fifteen, worked in loads the way ch 104 worked hers. **On the thirty-third day of the
  quarry she shuts the door of an empty hut at the third hour and says no out loud, to a stove, in the
  ordinary voice**, and it goes down in about eleven seconds. **No bracketed line. Nothing goes out of her
  into nine counties. No lamp, no keeper, no girl in a wash house, no sentence in the layer's voice.**
  What the filing records is an hour worked, certified and stamped irregular. **A suppressed stage leaves
  exactly the same absence as any other hole: the same width.** **Sun He's precedent holds it as an
  absence; a reader above this county reads absence as concealment, and he is right, and nothing stops
  it.** She gains the feeling of putting a heavy stone down and loses it to a man at the back of a room.
  **He has not asked her a question and he is not going to, and he says so in one breath so that she does
  not have to spend a morning on it.**
- **Canon change 51 — Ledger's standing (ch 160).** After the ch 149 retake he put it down the same day and
  **refuses permanently for the volume.** **From ch 150 Ledger is hers to order and his to hold only on her
  word.** Stated once on the page: *the standing is yours now and not mine… it was always yours… this
  time it is not me deciding that I will not. It is me not being allowed to.* **This is the first thing in
  the year that is different and she did it on purpose.**
- **Canon change 52 — the request on the shelf (ch 163).** A county office keeps a public shelf of
  everything that has gone up and **anybody may read it.** On it: **a request with a number and a date, for
  an instrument not held in this county, for use in this county, after the reading of the winter return.**
  Nothing sent, nothing filed, the answer space empty. **A field agent cannot unmake a System inside a
  bound without a Wall writ, so the only lawful thing he can do about it is write a line and let a clerk
  give it a number — which is the whole of the eleven weeks, and why he attended two readings, bought one
  thing off a county, and never asked her a question.** She does four pieces of arithmetic on the shelf and
  does none of them. **The referral itself is NOT filed and NOT spoken: that is ch 169–175.**

### Other Batch 0003 facts

- **Channel: about ten weeks at ch 140 going in; the price is named once at ch 149 and the remainder is
  stated there — about five and a half weeks — and about three weeks at ch 160**, against **eleven weeks
  of winter left in it.** **Ten minus four and a half is five and a half, and the gap between that and
  ch 160's three is thirty-six days of twice a day under her own ch 118 conditions; that arithmetic is
  now on the page at ch 160 and must not be "corrected" back to three.** **No bare Settlement fraction on
  the page.**
- **Merit: none.** The only Merit in Volume 3 is the ch 140 kill.
- **No paid work in this county for a licensed woman with one arm until the thaw**, except the quarry
  three miles east, which runs about six more weeks out of a tin of two hundred and thirty coppers.
- **Feng is warm for the first time in twenty-two years** (ch 157) and has the county's chair on the
  county's reading days. She says the one sentence she has been avoiding **out loud** — *I do not forgive
  you, and also you are sixty-two and warm* — and calls it a discount and says it is the only one she will
  give him in that tower. **He has not asked her a question since ch 143.**
- **Titles 149–163:** Carried Down the Stair, What Stops Talking, The Attendance, Slate and Chalk, The
  Monthly Reading, The Signing Hand, A Date in a Counting Room, Walking In, Nothing Chalked, The Weight
  Ticket, Before It Comes, The Second Hole, Where He Is Right, What Da Shan Told Him, Not Held in This
  County. **All unique against 1–148.**
- **Chapter length:** 30,961 words over 15 chapters, **average ~2,064**, counted after the Batch 0003
  review repair. Ch 150 at 2,863 is above the 1,800–2,700 guide and is the milestone; the other fourteen
  are inside or just below it; the shortest is ch 151 at 1,653. **Nothing under the 1,300 floor and no monotone decline. Do not read the
  average as a precedent.** Counts are body prose only.

### Carried forward, unchanged, into Batch 0004

Crew **nine men; quarters sleep ten in twelve bunks.** The girl of twenty-four alive, four paid hours,
leg set crooked, charged to the eighth estate, holding about two months of uncopied run lists, **now told
out loud that she has been put under a line and asked for it to be recorded between them.** Hou Sanliang
**unpaid, patient, never in a room with her, never met**; his counting room now holds a **date**. Shen Xiu
does not appear. The three sponsors do not appear and their purchased silence holds — **nothing about the
second book is lodged, copied or spoken anywhere in ch 119–178.** The register still reads *reconciled*;
**the plate is still worn**; the fourth stage of her life is on no paper in nine counties and never will be,
**and now the eighth is on no paper either**. *Kiln-born* in two heads and no paper; the office name
still unreadable and **not named in this batch**. Surety void, debt hers. The twelve-sheet frame logged
and untouched. **The tower intake line at the ninth hour is not audited and not examined as a change.**
**The Foundation writ is dead and not revoked and the relicensing did not revive it. Nothing in this batch
softens, defers or reopens the ch 150 refusal, and nothing in it can unwrite it.** **She does not forgive
him.**

chapter order, the card beats, the single irreversible decision, the zero-bracket budget and every ending
are unchanged. All repairs are word-level or one-sentence level. **The binding day table in
`outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0003.md` was upheld in every disputed case and the prose was moved onto it
— the day table is canon and the chapters had drifted away from it.**

## Established facts, Volume 3 Batch 0004 (ch 164–178) — COST AND SIGNATURE. VOLUME 3 CLOSED.

Fifteen chapters, **30,508 words**, average ~2,034. **Day 0 is the last day of the thaw.** This batch runs
**day 187 to day 219.** Chapter days: 164 = 187 · 165 = 189 · 166 = 191 · 167 = 194 · 168 = 196 ·
169 = 197 · 170 = 197 (later) · 171 = 198 · 172 = 203 · 173 = 209 · 174 = 212 · 175 = 213 ·
176 = 215 · 177 = 217 · 178 = 219.

**The month-day table, fixed at review and canon.** Ch 150 puts day 136 at **the third of the month**, so
**months run thirty days from a first at day 134 and month-day = `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`.** (This block used
to read *month-day = day − 133*, which is an offset true only for day 134 to day 163; every date stated below is
correct under the rule above.) The three monthly attendances are **day 147, day 178 and
day 209 = the sixteenth**; **the winter return is read at the second hour on day 212 = the nineteenth**;
the petition is filed day 213 = the twentieth; the crate goes up the twentieth; the district's slip comes
down two days after; nine men read the schedule in the Hall on **the twenty-second**. Ch 164 and ch 165
carried no month-day at all. **A draft read fourteenth → seventeenth; it was moved to the binding day
table, and the fourteen-day gap between the announcement (day 198) and the referral (day 212) is now the
only elapsed figure either chapter needs.**

**Power: Condensing 8 → 9 at ch 171, ANNOUNCED at her own order, WITNESSED BY THE CREW, at the cold end,
at the ninth hour. Nothing else. No tribulation. EXACTLY ONE bracketed layer line in the whole batch, and
it is at ch 171:**

[Assembly Hall of Qinghe. Ning Yan of the Qinghe furnace crew, servant-born, under a ceiling written in her own hand. What is carried past the seventh is let go. Condensing, stage nine, at Lowwater is held.]

Third clause fresh against ch 135 (*on a licence whose ceiling is nine*) and ch 148 (*entered upward through
the Lantern Register of this county*); the fourth clause advances the ordinal by the stage. **Not a reuse.**

### Cost and signature (ch 164–178)

- **The quarry (ch 164–165).** Eleven men cut to **five** — four on the haul, one on the hoist. The face
  has **four days of stone** a bar can reach and then nothing until the thaw. Bo asks one question in the
  whole run. **Kan has forty-one cards** (canon change 48, unchanged) and she forecasts **forty-three** —
  one a day the rope runs, and the binding day table leaves exactly two days. **Her hour is at the left of
  thirty-four of them.** The pay-off is counted in the open at the rope: **a hundred and seventy loads at
  twelve coppers = two thousand and forty gross; a third went at the hand = six hundred and eighty; one
  thousand three hundred and sixty in her hand.** Twelve coppers is canon (ch 154, 157, 160, 163) —
  eighteen before the hand, and **the hand does not go back**. The tin goes from about two hundred and
  thirty to **about one thousand five hundred and ninety**.
- **Canon change 53 — the stone, and what she has been buying (ch 166).** The cut charged **twelve**. The
  only stone left is **a bank up the east road at nine coppers a load**; the yard that sells by the load is
  **four days east** and the season's road shut on day 117. **She has been buying the stone, not the loads**
  — the carrying is four men and their boots, and four of her crew do it on their own time. **Da Shan says
  out loud that he has been charging her five and is going to charge nine**, and that if she had offered to
  stop he would have said yes. She keeps buying. **Six weeks × four loads = 216 coppers; the four coppers
  a load is 96 of that, and 96 is the whole price of the decision.**
- **Canon change 54 — the cold end is not hers (ch 167).** **Nobody has the standing to post a woman to
  the cold end for a season, and nobody has posted her.** She signs for her own shift every morning and
  walks forty feet past **nine men**. Ten days and nineteen shifts without one of them coming to that end.
  **The crew's silence was distance, not respect, and it had been distance since the schedule went on the
  board.** The fifth week of five builds nine; the ninth is not an address the way the eighth was, it is
  loud and everywhere, and there is no door in that building for it.
- **Canon change 55 — the notice and the registrar's line (ch 168).** **A notice has a rule behind it and an
  order has a person's.** The winter return is read at the second hour on the nineteenth and a member whose
  hours are on it stands in the room while they are read. **Under it, written by a registrar and not by a
  rule: a member attending may be shown the sheet before it is read, and may ask for an hour not to be read
  to be entered as read and not as absent.** That line is for one person or for everybody, and a registrar
  does not write lines for everybody. **She goes.** Cost of going: four miles each way, a morning in four,
  the third flight with three counts, and a morning of the girl's bad leg. Cost of not going: a column
  with three holes in it, read into a room that decides.
- **Canon change 56 — Qin (ch 169, 171, 172).** **NEW MINOR CHARACTER: Qin, twenty-two, two winters on the
  crew.** He comes to the cold end on the last morning of the fifth week and stands in it and does not work,
  and **she does not send him back.** He has watched eight other men get good at leaving a room and has
  worked out that the leaving has a shape. **He never asks her the question.** He writes nothing down, is
  not thanked, is not forgiven, does not become a plot, and is on the floor at the announcement with Gao.
  By ch 172 he has walked four miles down the mountain four times to stand eight feet off in a queue while
  she writes a column, and says he was going to keep doing it.
- **Canon change 57 — the tower, and the stair carried by another hand (ch 170, 176, 178).** **Feng is warm
  for the first time in twenty-two years, sixty-two, coughing less, writing more, owned, a Listener for
  life.** The upward bundle goes up four flights in a district clerk's arms and comes down empty, **because
  Feng asked for the stair to be taken off him to be written as permanent.** He has copied all four county
  copies of her stage column in fair hand — one, two, three, two ruled lines, five, six, seven, two ruled
  lines — **for the building, not the county.** **He cannot read what is in the gaps and has not been able
  to for nine years**, and he has thought about it for four weeks and done nothing, and calls that the
  correct answer and not much of one. **He has not asked her a question since ch 143 and asks none here.**
  He tells her to stand at the mark **before** the ninth hour, not at it: the setting comes out at the ninth
  and nine men working is the same nine men and costs her nothing.
- **Canon change 58 — the announcement, and its price (ch 171).** **Condensing 9, at her own order, in the
  open, at the cold end, in front of nine men.** **It takes the space with it** — she is full, top to the
  soles of her feet, with no gap anywhere in her anywhere, and she has never in her life been that — and it
  is over before the setting comes back out of the mark. **Her holding-or-telling decision is settled on the
  page and is not re-opened:** telling puts her exact position in nine counties **fourteen days before a
  referral carrying her name, her stages and her hours in order goes up the stair**; holding it a fourth
  time gives the column a fourth hole, which the county rules an absence and an office above this county
  reads as concealment, **and she would then be doing the thing she does in order to be suspected of doing
  it.** **She tells the crew, out loud, at the mark, on the day, rather than letting nine men work it out
  off a board over a fortnight.** The price is also in the room: **the cold end fills with people and stays
  filled and is never hers again.**
- **Canon change 59 — the hand said out loud (ch 172).** Nine men have noticed: Gao stopped asking her to
  hold the slate; Qin walks to the counter; **a young clerk offers to write the column for her and is
  refused**, because eleven minutes in her hand is four minutes in a clerk's and then there are two hands on
  it. **At the ninth hour on a furnace floor, in front of all nine, she says: the heel of her left hand is
  gone, a third of every load goes and does not come back, the county column takes eleven minutes where it
  used to take four.** Gao's whole response is *then say so at the frame and I will hold it*, and he holds
  it. **Nobody thanks her because she did not tell them to and would have been furious if they had.**
- **Canon change 60 — the licence has nothing left on it (ch 173).** **A county has to enter a stage that
  was announced** — date, place, and the fact that the member made it, or it is a different document. Then
  Sun He stops and does arithmetic. **The ceiling is nine; she is at nine; there is nothing above nine on
  the document.** A second relicensing needs a schedule of hours worked at the ceiling being asked for, and
  **this county does not keep a schedule of work done past its boundary — not kept elsewhere, not kept at
  all**, because past the boundary the work is done by people this county does not employ, examine, or hold
  a second hand for. **The licence she fought for in the summer is finished the moment she reaches the top
  of it, and that is not a punishment; it is what a document is.**
- **Canon change 61 — the reading, in order (ch 174).** The winter return is read **in public, at the second
  hour, with a queue out into the square**. The column reads **one, two, three, five, six, seven, nine**.
  Each of the three gaps is ruled in the margin with the county's four words, *No stage entered for this
  county at this date*, and **Member present** is written at the foot. **Sun He states the ruling aloud and
  stops, because he has said it three times in nineteen months and there is no fourth sentence in this
  county better than the first three.** **She signs it herself — eleven minutes with the flat of a second
  finger, the first figure about fifty seconds and the ninth about twenty, in front of a queue that does not
  shuffle** — and every person in the room can see the shape of what is not there on her left hand.
- **Canon change 62 — the certification and the referral (ch 174).** **In a Warden's hand, signed by the
  office and not by a person, read twice, in front of a registrar, a clerk and two members of the public:**
  the thing in her head is **real**; it is **unauthorised**; it is **carried by a licensee in good
  standing** whose licence, ceiling, conditions and defect stand and **whose seventh condition travels with
  her and is not answered by this instrument**. **Then, spoken, not written: a field man of a Warden's
  office cannot unmake a System inside the bound without a Wall writ, there is no such writ in this county,
  and he is referring the dismantling upward to the Warden.** The referral **carries her name, her stages in
  order and her hours, and carries no agent's name.** **The request on the public shelf goes live and goes on
  the crate with it, four weeks after it was written.** He bought one certified copy and a receipt off that
  counter and nothing else.
- **Canon change 63 — the Foundation petition (ch 175).** Four lines on Hall paper: a relicensing with the
  ceiling at **Foundation, the first stage**, on a relicensed licence with seven conditions, grounded on an
  hours record continuous from the origin date and held in this county in full. **She folds the filing copy
  of her own licence in behind the four lines, front outward**, so the seventh condition goes up the stair
  with the petition — and because a thing nobody has looked at cannot be refused. **Entered, routed,
  barred — NOT refused** — with the ground of the bar entered as the absence of a schedule and **not** the
  standing of the applicant, and a last line written slower than the rest: ***the answer to this application
  is not this office's to write.*** **Sun He takes the clerk's pen, not his own, which is the difference
  between a thing entered and a thing ruled. Once entered it cannot be withdrawn by her**, and it is dated
  the day after a referral went up out of this county, so both papers ride the same crate. **Foundation 1
  belongs to Volume 4.**
- **Canon change 64 — what went up, and what came down (ch 176).** One crate, one stair: the quarterly
  schedule with *irregular* on the front for the fourth time this year, the referral, the request, and her
  petition. **The district's answer to the request: received, numbered, referred upward, no instrument held
  in the district either, and an empty space the width of a line where an answer was meant to go.** Nine men
  read the schedule in the Hall and **Gao does the sum out loud, on purpose, in a hall, so that nine men
  have heard it from a mouth instead of off a wall.** **Da Shan says a man came in on the twelfth and asked
  how many loads came down that week, and he told him, because it is his board.** The board behind a well has
  **eight frames, two of which were off and are back.** **The tin is about a hundred and forty coppers.**
- **Canon change 65 — the licence comes out of the oilcloth (ch 177, 178).** She takes it out **on the bench
  in the wash house**, reads the front and looks at the last line of the back **without reading the seven
  conditions, because she read them once**, and does not tear off the eight hundred jin. **The Clay Plate
  stays against her ribs**; the register still reads *reconciled*. **The first licence comes down off two
  nails**, going soft at the crease of a defect line she wrote herself, and is rolled into her coat lining
  before it is unreadable. Four nails, six coppers. **She practises in the dark at the foot of the bunk —
  three straight out of five, the fifth splitting the pine — and then in daylight in the yard behind the
  Hall, where the second hour teaches her the third thing: the hand does not want to be watched, and the
  stroke is something she has been doing rather than letting the arm finish.** **At the seventh hour, with
  nine men coming in off the shift, she nails the second licence up straight above her own bunk. Ten
  sleepers, twelve bunks, and the tower lamp burning four miles off.**
- **Canon change 66 — the final image (ch 178).** **First week of the deep cold.** She asks how much of it is
  there and he tells her the truth: **about ten days** — and about six weeks of winter still to come in it.
  **The Ledger is hers to order and she does not order it**, and no price is named anywhere in this batch.
  **She does not forgive him. She does not tell him to stop.**

  less the eleven chapters to the close is about ten days; **that arithmetic stays where Batch 0003 left it

  advance, gains four coppers a week out of a district fund for the cold months because she wrote the

- **Titles 164–178:** The Last Card, The Pay-Off, Nine Coppers a Load, The Cold End, The Notice, The Man

### Construction budget — spent in Batch 0004, not to reuse

The seven relicensing conditions re-listed: **zero.** The claim's position on the paper: **used once, at
ch 177, as a thing looked at.** The suppression arithmetic re-run: **zero** — the three gaps are ruled with
four words and Sun He stops. The signed-shift roster rule **re-explained: zero** (a draft restated the ch
60 rule twice in ch 167; both were cut and the consequence kept). *There is no form*: **zero.
*There is no column*: **zero.** *The record remained*: **zero.** *Nobody asked*: **zero.** *Nobody knew*:
**one, at ch 164, doing real work** — nobody in nine counties knows what her hand is. *Nobody was thanked*:
**one, at ch 172**, and it is the one place the construction is allowed because the girl has spent two
volumes setting not-being-thanked up as protection. **Nobody said anything: reduced from six to three and
spaced across three chapters.** The four seconds: **one use at ch 171 plus the one-word beat**, against
three at ch 135 and ch 148. `his eyes widened`, `he couldn't believe it`, `suddenly`, `little did he know`:
**zero.** Weekdays: **zero named anywhere in the batch** — two were caught in draft at ch 168 and cut.
Authorial scaffolding: **zero**; *this chapter*, *this volume*, *the reader*, *the whole series*, and the
word *batch*: **zero.** **No Wall described, no crossing, no geography past a marker post, no second
outside-bound office, no new final enemy.**

`logs/volume-03-batch-0004.review.log` swept ch 119–178 and found two claims in the state files that the

- **No weekday is named anywhere in ch 119–178.** `Thursday` stood ten times: ch 123 ×4, ch 128 ×4,
  ch 131 ×2. **All ten are out.** **The crate rule that ch 128 builds and ch 131 pays off survives without
  the name — the bundle came up *on the day the crate went*, the sponsors seal *the day after the crate
  goes*, and Deng Chao read that line *in the morning of the day the crate went*.** ch 123's four became
  *by the end of the week* and *inside the week*. Nothing else in the volume was touched.
- **The *eleven weeks* figure now stands at ch 150, 162, 163 and 174 only.** It was traded for a relative
  count at **ch 153** (*in all the weeks of this cold*, twice, keeping his exact echo), **ch 161** (*at a
  counter in this room since the thaw*; *had bought one thing off a county and nothing since*) and
  **ch 173** (*at any counter in this county*, which also removes a doubled construction thirty lines from
  *I have asked you nothing in eleven weeks*). **The two chapters where it was flatly wrong against the day
  table are ch 153 (day 147) and ch 161 (day 178), and neither states it now.** It is left where it is the
  subject rather than a tic: **ch 163 accounts for it in full** and **ch 174's *learned eleven weeks earlier*
  is exact against day 133 → 212.**

### Carried forward, unchanged, into Volume 4

**A Warden's office outside the bound now holds her name, her stages in order and her hours, certified by
her own county.** Crew **nine men; ten sleepers in twelve bunks.** The girl of twenty-four alive, four paid
hours plus four from a district fund for the cold months. Hou Sanliang **unpaid, patient, never in a room
with her, never met**; his counting room holds a **date**. Shen Xiu unforgiven, unthanked. The three
sponsors silent; **nothing about the second book is lodged, copied or spoken anywhere in ch 119–178.**
The register still reads *reconciled*; **the plate is still worn.** *Kiln-born* in two heads and no paper;
**the office name still unreadable and still not named.** The fourth and eighth stages of her life and
nothing else are on no paper. **The Foundation writ is dead and not revoked and the relicensing did not
revive it; the second relicensing is petitioned, numbered, barred and not withdrawable, and only an office
above this county can complete it.** **The second licence is now nailed up straight over the second bunk;
the first is rolled in her coat lining.** The tower intake line at the ninth hour is not audited and is not
examined as a change. **She does not forgive him.** **Ledger is hers to order and his to hold only on her
word, and she has about ten days of channel.**
